# Regulatory and gender policy framework of CsICAP Project

## International Instruments

Concerning gender equity and the guarantee of rights of women, Colombia has signed and ratified several international instruments. First, it is worth mentioning the 1979 Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination against Women - CEDAW (approved by Colombia through Law 51 of 1981), which confirmed the country's commitment to the pursuit of gender equality in all areas of life.

Additionally, the country has participated in different international forums, such as the First International Conference on Women (Mexico, 1975), where the Decade for Women plan was signed; the Second World Conference on Women (Copenhagen, 1980), which emphasized the elimination of discrimination against women and girls; the Third World Conference on Women (Beijing, 1995); and the Fourth World Conference on Women (Beijing, 1995), which established a platform of action for the rights of women.


---
**Page 133**
---

The above-mentioned conventions and conferences share the characteristic of recognizing the link between gender inequalities and the disproportionate effects of climate change on women, raising awareness of the need to adopt measures that are consistent with this situation and that improve the quality of life of women, while at the same time adopting measures to protect and restore ecosystems.

Likewise, there are regional platforms in which Colombia has participated, such as the Convention for the Prevention, Punishment, and Eradication of Violence against Women (Convention of Belém do Pará), which was ratified by Law 248 of 1995 and creates a regional instrument for the recognition of the full and free exercise of civil, political, economic, social, and cultural rights. The Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination against Women and the Caribbean, organized by ECLAC, appeals to States to guarantee the participation of rural, indigenous, and Afro-descendant women in political life and paid work.

Moreover, the Colombian government has signed a series of United Nations resolutions that are related to gender equality, women's empowerment, and their political participation: Resolution 60/216 (2005) and Resolution 66/130 (2012). Particularly, Resolutions 62/136 (2008) and 64/140 (2009) are aimed at improving the situation of women in rural areas.

Recently, the country has aligned itself with the Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) as a reference point for the formulation of public policy in the country. In particular, SDG 5 refers to gender equality and calls on States to eliminate all forms of discrimination against women and girls; other goals such as climate action, underwater life, and terrestrial ecosystem life can be addressed from a gender perspective.

### 4.2 National regulations and public policy

There are two main groups of gender equity regulations at the national level: one focused on the protection of the rights of women and the other with special emphasis on rural women, their productive inclusion, and, in general, on creating tools for gender equity in rural areas of the country.

Among the main laws that protect women's rights are Article 43 of the 1991 Political Constitution, which guarantees equal rights and opportunities between men and women; Law 823 of 2001, which dictates norms on equal opportunities for women; Law 1232 of 2008, which defines the National System for the Promotion of Women's Participation in Decision-Making; Law 1459 of 2010, which guarantees that at least 30% of women in public decision-making positions and calls for the adoption of similar measures in the private sector and civil society. This law has become a reference framework for the establishment of affirmative measures related to the minimum representation of women in government programs, for example, those related to productive projects.

Regarding the participation of women in decision-making spaces, Law 581 of 2003, or the Quota Law, establishes that at least 30% of women in public decision-making positions and calls for the adoption of similar measures in the private sector and civil society. This law has become a reference framework for the establishment of affirmative measures related to the minimum representation of women in government programs, for example, those related to productive projects.

23


---
**Page 134**
---

In 2013, through document CONESI 145, the National Public Policy on Gender Equity was adopted to contribute to guarantee the full enjoyment of the rights of Colombian women. To this end, it promotes the mainstreaming of the gender approach in all sectors of the economy, giving special attention to rural women.

Law 731 of 2002, or the Rural Women’s Law, aims to promote the empowerment of rural women and prioritizes low-income rural women. This is based on the definition of affirmative and favorable measures for women, which include: i) access to financing (from which the Credit Line for Low-Income Rural Women was created), ii) the titling of agrarian reform lands equitably for women and their spouses; and iii) participation (of at least 50%) in reforestation and environmental restoration projects.

On December 16, 2015, Law 1876 of 2015 was signed, creating the Directorate of Rural Women in the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, responsible for ensuring the welfare of rural women.

On women’s right to land ownership, there are several norms. Decree-Law No. 902 of 2017 establishes that women can be co-owners with their husbands or common-law spouses or permanent partners and recognizes care activities as uses for the use of rural lands for land adjudication. In turn, Law 1500 establishes that the Family Agricultural Units on vacant land will be awarded to women who are heads of households.

Similarly, Law 1486/2011 or Victim Law established that women have special protection from the State in procedures associated with land restitution, such as preferential access to credit, training, and social security programs.

It is also worth mentioning that, regarding productive activities, Article 8 of Law 1908 of 2008 establishes that the gender approach must be included and guarantee priority access to rural women. In this sense, the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development has implemented several programs related to productive projects of the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development and other entities that favor rural activity. Similarly, for productive projects, the aforementioned article 8 of Law 1908 of 2008 establishes that the gender approach must be included in this sense; the Regulations of the Comprehensive Agricultural and Rural Development Projects (IDARs) for its acronym in Spanish) of the Rural Development Agency, adopted by Agreement 634 of 2015, also establish that women are part of the five population groups subject to differential attention.

In terms of agricultural extension, Law 1876 of 2017 created the National Agricultural Innovation System (SNA for its acronym in Spanish) and defines the public agricultural extension service as a service provided by the State through the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development. This service includes technical assistance that includes the sustainable management of natural resources and the promotion of practices for mitigating and adapting to climate change. In this context, the law establishes


---
**Page 135**
---

affirmative and favorable measures for rural women, such as their prioritization as beneficiaries of the subsidy for the public agricultural extension service fee, the promotion of the development of organization of female producers, and their participation in the SNIA's Superior Council through a rural woman representative.

Another important norm regarding the welfare of rural women is Resolution 846 of 2017 on public policy guidelines for Peasant, Family and Community Agriculture (PFCF) for its acronym in Spanish. This resolution was approved by the National Congress and entered into force in the ACCF, which constitutes 75% of the country's Agricultural Units.

Moreover, the Basis of the National Development Plan (PNB for its acronym in Spanish) 2018-2022, throughout Chapter XV presents the Pact for the Equity of Women, whose objective is the creation of a budget line for women and the inclusion of gender equality as an indicator in the national system through the Intersectoral Care of the Common Sense, recognizing the need for differential policies for rural women. Furthermore, the PNB 2018-2022 is a pioneer in the creation of a budget line for women, which will be implemented from 2019 onwards and which will receive resources from the General Budget of the Nation have identified their investments and commitments to close the gaps between men and women, both in investment and operation.

In line with the roadmap established in the PNB 2018-2022, state strategies to address rural women and their needs have been promoted and implemented by entities at the national public level. These include the National Gender Equality Plan (PNEG for its acronym in Spanish) of the Directorate for Rural Women, as well as the National Land Agency (ANF for its acronym in Spanish) and the Rural Development Agency (ADR for its acronym in Spanish), and in some cases, the Ministry of Agriculture and Livestock (MAGA). The strategies have been developed under the 2013 National Public Policy on Gender Equity for Women, which is composed by five strategic axes: a) Access to land and credit; b) Ownership and access to assets; c) Participation in policy-making and decision-making scenarios; d) Sexual and reproductive health and rights; e) Gender focus in education; and, finally, f) A life free of violence. The National Gender Equality Plan (PNEG) is coordinated by the National Advisory Office for Women's Equity also articulated with entities at the national and territorial levels, promoting the creation of territorial links to promote gender-focused policy in the territories. This has been done through the implementation of development projects, the signing of pacts, the expansion of coverage, and the integration of resources in the areas of associativity, technology, access to land, technical assistance, among others.

Among the recently implemented programs, Colombia Siembra stands out, launched from 2015 to 2018, which proposed to increase the supply of agricultural products for the agroindustry and promote the development of women in rural areas. In this program, which is part of the production chain, one of its objectives was to historically increase the support and participation of women in the country's agricultural sector. Thus, Colombia Siembra favored nearly 506,264 women with 506 Mujeres Rurales (Mujer Rural) and 100 Mujeres Familiares, 37% of which are on the responsibility of women heads of household (Políticas Públicas Sector Agro Alimentario a Mis de 506 Mf Mujeres Rurales / Fugnaro, n.d.).

25


---
**Page 136**
---

More recently, in October 2019, the Pact for the Equity of Rural Women was signed between the Government, trade unions, and organizations of rural women, as well as unions and companies in the agricultural production sector. This pact aims to shorten the poverty and social exclusion gaps of women in the Colombian countryside, giving priority to low-income women, and empowering them through economic empowerment.

The Pact proposes five commitments to be achieved in the short and medium-term: 1. Economic empowerment for the elimination of gaps faced by women compared with men in terms of income, assets, access to productive resources, and land; 2. Sexual and reproductive rights; 4. Rights of women to a life free of violence and, finally, 5. The strengthening of gender institutions for rural women. Within the framework of this Pact, the Rural Women’s Fund was created in 2019 with the aim of promoting the empowerment of women of the country with the co-financing of Comprehensive Agricultural and Rural Development Plans, in projects in the areas of cocoa, potatoes, milk, beans, bananas, machinery banks, and agricultural credit. In addition, the creation of a network for the promotion of women in marketing and farmer’s market have been supported. Also, technical assistance interventions reached 6,813 users; strategies for the promotion, participation, and strengthening associated benefits to 4,339 women.

The generation of income through economic independence has also been a relevant axis within the implementation of strategies by the national government. The MADR and its affiliated entities have offered programs that have established affirmative measures in the prioritization of groups of women and associations as beneficiaries, to increase their participation of this population in the productive process. An example is the work carried out with “Comunidades Capacidad Empresarial Rurales” (Building Rural Entrepreneurial Capacities) within the framework of the El Campo Program (Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, 2019). This program has been implemented in 35 municipalities representing 58.2% of the people served, similarly, “la Generación de Ingresos y Desarrollo de Capacidad Productiva” (Income Generation and Development of Productive Capacities) project was carried out with women from the departments of Antioquia, Cauca, Huila, and Tolima to commercialize agricultural products (Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, 2020). Likewise, “Oportunidades Pacíficas Mujeres Rurales” (Pleasant Opportunities for Rural Women in the Pacific Region) is a program that seeks to strengthen women’s organizations in the Colombian Pacific, strengthen their capacities, and provide opportunities to generate empowerment in the context of the COVID-19 pandemic that affected the country. Aimed at organizing women from 20 municipalities in the departments of Cauca, Valle del Cauca, Chocó, and Nariño. This program is supported by the Korean International Cooperation Agency and the United Nations Development Program (UNDP). The project seeks to provide resources to the women involved (Minagricultura Lanza Proyectos “Oportunidades Pacíficas Mujeres Rurales” Que Fortalecen Organizaciones de Mujeres Productoras de la Región Del Pacifico Colombiano, n.d.).

Among the financial instruments offered to improve the access of women to land and productive inputs, there are offers through financial intermediaries (such as Banco Agrario), agricultural credit with special conditions, guarantees to back them, and coverage up to 100% of the direct costs of the project, to finance activities that are essential to the development of the productive


---
**Page 137**
---

project, whether in the agricultural or fishing sector, as well as mining, rural tourism and handcraft projects. Among other things, labor expenses, infrastructure construction, purchase of machinery and equipment, inputs, and purchase of livestock may be financed. Among the institution's financial instruments there are the Credit Line for Low-Income Rural Women and the Special Agricultural Credit Line (LAC). The interest rate on these lines is 10% E.A., however, the National Agricultural Credit Commission defines the LECI for the respective year, among which an additional interest rate subsidy of between 0.5% and 1.0% E.A. is usually granted to rural enterprises.

Concerning efforts to increase land tenure by rural women, the National Land Agency has strategies for the adjudication of vacant land, formalization of rural land, formalization to replace illicit crops, comprehensive land subsidies, among others. In 2019, ANT benefited more than 5,600 women, 47% of the total beneficiaries of the programs. During this year, ANT awarded 38,000 hectares of land to rural women and allocated 50% of the subsidies to rural women to gain access to land ownership and develop productive projects (Agencia Nacional de Tierras Despliega Beneficios Para La Mujer Rural – Agencia Nacional de Tierras).

Additionally, the Special Administrative Unit for the Management of Restitution of Land Restitution (UJR in its acronym in Spanish) is responsible for facilitating the restitution of land to the owners or possessors of land or exploiters of vacant land who have been victims of dispossession. In 2019, UJR implemented a program that aimed at ensuring that women received a program of special access to women to the restitution process, to guarantee women's right to land during the restitution process. This intervention was implemented so that women could "reappropriate" their rights over the land and thus be able to exercise their ownership of the land". The results obtained so far show 52% of the women belonging to the process as beneficiaries, with 35.5% of productive projects headed by women, and the training of 16,423 women in the framework of the program "Reconstruyendo el hogar" (Restitution of Tierras Empoderada a las Mujeres Victimas - Banco de Fomento – Fondo Público), p.45.

| International | Convention on the Rights of Migrant Workers (CRMW) | States that migrant workers have the right to respect for their inherent dignity and worth; that they are entitled to equal treatment before the law as well as the protection of the law, including the right to an effective remedy by competent national tribunals or bodies in accordance with the law. It also points out the importance of the role of rural women, so often neglected in legislation, regarding the recognition that this is vital for the development of the countryside. |

### 4.4 Institutional capacities at the national and local levels

Compliance with the norms and implementation of the policies and programs indicated in the previous section is ensured by the Ministry of Agriculture, Rural Development, Fishing and Rural Development Sector, which is made up of the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development and its many affiliated and related entities, which participate in different stages of the construction of the national rural development strategy. The gender perspective implies the constant articulation of different agencies in each of the entities. Precisely, the responsibility for leading this articulation lies with the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development, which is responsible for the implementation of Law 731 of 2005 (Law of Rural Women) as a technical directorate created in the Vice-Ministry of Rural Development in 2015 and its guiding axis is the implementation of Law 731 of 2005 (Law of Rural Women) in the areas of competence of the sector.


---
**Page 140**
---

The main function of the DMR is the coordination, design, and evaluation of policy plans and projects aimed at influencing the social and economic well-being of rural women, as well as promoting coordination with national and territorial entities for the implementation of such plans and projects. At the national level, the main interlocutors of the DMR are the High Council for Rural Development (HCRRD), the National Agency for Land Administration (ANT) related to rural development and gender equity, and the entities attached to and linked to the Ministry.

At the territorial level, the main interlocutors are the territorial governments (departments and municipalities), the regional directors of agriculture and rural development secretaries, or whoever acts as the liaison for gender issues in the territories. The articulated work between national entities allows, among other things, the follow-up of the gender indicators of the sector in the National Development Plan and the Implementation Framework Plan. The monitoring and follow-up of these indicators makes it possible to provide feedback on the progress made by each entity.

The coordination between the entities allows the formulation of joint strategies for the productive inclusion of rural women along the value chain of the agricultural and rural development sectors. This value chain includes the identification, design, and formalization of land to marketing strategies and the generation of added economic value.

In the area of access and formalization of land ownership, the National Land Agency (ANT for its acronym in Spanish), through the gender team in the General Directorate, has managed to incorporate strategies that promote the participation of rural women in the land restitution process. The ANT is responsible for implementing agricultural policy and productive inclusion, has incorporated strategies for the participation of rural women in the different services it offers. Within the ADR, the Participation and Association Unit (UAP) is responsible for promoting the participation of rural women in the land restitution process; however, there are gender liaisons in the national directorates and territorial offices under the responsibility of the Productive Integration Vice Presidency, through which the agency implements strategies that promote the participation of rural women, unions, territorial entities and other entities to promote the participation of rural women in agricultural production.

It should be recalled that the ANT and the ADR are the main executors of the Ministry of Agriculture and agricultural policy of Rural Development, for which they have offices in the territory. The DMR has a presence in the 32 provinces through its territorial units, known as thirteen Territorial Technical Units, which reflects their weak territorial presence in the 32


---
**Page 141**
---

departments of the country. In addition, the gender liaisons in these offices are usually in charge of multiple issues and responsibilities, which limits the capacity of the agencies to address gender issues in practice.

FINAGRO and the Agrario Bank do not have units exclusively dedicated to gender mainstreaming, but multiple units are working to create affirmative measures to promote the financial inclusion of rural women. In the case of FINAGRO, the presidential advisory team has designed a periodic report on the status of women in the sector, which includes monitoring actions with the areas of FINAGRO and the different actors of the National Agricultural Credit Commission. Agrario Bank also has people charge of issues of rural women in the small productive units.

The Rural Development Unit (UDR) is an institution that belongs to the Agrario Bank, a financial institution with the largest territorial presence, covering 10.84% of the country's municipalities through offices and banking correspondents.

Finally, the Rural Agricultural and Livestock Planning Unit (URPA, for its acronym in Spanish), the entity responsible for planning rural development and rural sector, is building the Comprehensive Agricultural Risk Management System, an initiative to centralize information that seeks to anticipate potential risks in the sector in different areas, one of which is the well-being of rural women. The UDR has a team with specific training and technical expertise in gender issues who are identifying gender rights in several prioritized production chains.

Finally, at the national level, the role of the Rural Development Directorate and the Gender Sub directorate of the National Planning Department in providing technical support to the Ministry and its attached or related entities is highlighted. Especially for the development of plans and policies that have an impact on the well-being of rural women and improve their productive participation in the sector.

As mentioned above, the entities responsible for implementing the national policy in the territory are the Secretariats of Women and the Secretariats of Agriculture, whose specific functions are to promote gender equality. In the case of the Secretariats of Agriculture, one of the Secretariats of Women, their efforts are generally oriented towards the formulation and direction of programs that have an impact on improving the welfare of women in different fields of social and productive life, as well as promoting their participation in territorial productive development. There are some specific cases, such as that of the Secretariat of Women of the Government of Cundinamarca, which has a unit specializing in issues of rural women. In this case, the Secretariats of Agriculture of the departments where it is located should be implemented also have Secretariats of Women: Antioquia, Arauca, Caucá, Córdoba, HUILA, Meta, Narino, Norte de Santander, Sucre, Tolima and Valle del Cauca. In other words, 12 (55%) of 22 departments would count on these secretariats as strategy allies for the implementation of the CISGAP project.

It is important to point out the role of the Secretariat of Agriculture in the implementation of income generation programs of the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development such as Support for Small Producers (SSP) and the National Program for Family Farming. These entities are responsible for promoting the call for proposals at the territorial level and disseminating affirmative measures in favor of rural women. In turn, it is the Secretariats of Agriculture that


---
**Page 142**
---

coordinate with the regional offices of the National Land Agency and the Rural Development Agency for the development of local activities such as business fairs, markets of farmers, and business rounds.

3.2.1 Design and implement a training and socialization plan on women’s empowerment and participation at all levels of the agricultural sector based on the following topics:

- Leadership, gender, and inequality
- Gender equality
- Local, regional, and national decision-making bodies and positions
- Associations
- Awareness raising among organizations, associations, or businesses

3.2.2 In addition, design and implement a plan to promote the participation of rural women in local and regional decision-making bodies at all levels of the agricultural sector, that base with local and regional authorities (mayor’s office, district directorate) for the promotion of women’s empowerment and information on calls for applications for decision-making positions, work experience, etc. Special emphasis will be placed on the participation of women in instances such as the Agricultural Technical Advisory Committee.

### 3.1.2. Designing and delivering training modules with men and women producers participating in the project for gender equality and gender mainstreaming (gender role, etc.), on the following topics:
- The care economy
- Small-scale agriculture
- Gender violence (motion, public services, among others)
- Women’s rights
- Economic and financial literacy (household and farm financial management, savings, loans, credit, income generation, and payment of activities carried out by women, preparing food for day laborers, etc.)
- A specific module for young rural women about sexual and reproductive rights

This strategy will be supported by the workshops already being developed to improve the economic and financial situation of women producers in the care economy for rural products, and the economic and financial situation of men and women producers in small-scale agriculture.

### 3.1.2. Designing and delivering a training module for women producers (women only activities for the care economy) on the use of new technologies provided by the Project, developed and delivered

| Training modules with men and women producers participating in the project for gender equality, mainstreaming, developed and delivered | Producer Associations, CAT, Gender Team, Rural Agriculture (Rural Directors) | $335,330 |
|---|---|---|
| Training module for women producers (women only activities for the care economy) on the use of new technologies provided by the Project, developed and delivered | Pre and during operation | Gender Team, Producer Associations | $287,360 |

**Page 155**

3.1.1. Design and implement a gender sensitive plan specifying the strategies for the inclusion of women in training activities and women’s access to technology and improved agricultural services, designed and implemented.
The plan includes:
- Community based awareness raising activities (including core work)
- Training and pertinent communication methods for the entire population
- Follow-up visits to ensure that women are included and that their training process is completed
- Provision of necessary tools (those who take care of them during project activities must receive payment for this work)

3.1.4. Design and implement a gender awareness training plan with agricultural extension workers, designed and implemented.
This will include awareness raising on Gender Based Violence and the importance of women’s participation in agriculture.
Rural Development is already working on rights, gender and the environment.

3.1.5. Design and implement a training and certification plan for women to increase their demand for agricultural extension services from the Producer Associations and other service providers, designed and implemented.
The plan will emphasize support strategies for the provision of quality rural women extension services.

3.2.1. Develop participatory work sessions with women to discuss the barriers they face in accessing credit facilities and other services that are difficult in accessing technical assistance and recommendations to promote their participation in agricultural production (including agro-processing), which serves as input for action 3.1.3 component.
The focus will be on identifying the specific needs of groups of women based on their ethnicity and age.

3.2.2. Develop local plans with women’s organizations to promote the participation of women (farmers) farmers’ groups or organizations that promote women in agriculture, designed and implemented.
These plans aim at:
- a tool to share knowledge and information regarding market access and credit facilities
- processes that ensure the sustainability of the improvements strategies. This will be done through the implementation of action 3.1.3 component.

3.2.3 Implement measures to share information regarding and engage women in agricultural extension services provided by the Ministry of Agriculture and financing such as the Special Credit Unit (SCU) for rural development, credit facilities, financial inclusion and literacy.
These will be done through existing rural service delivery programs that target their population group as well as the courses in Economic and Financial Education available to the Ministry of Agriculture.

3.5.3 Fedegan, Fereshteh: Design the Gender Action Plan that mainstreams gender within the Association’s policies and practices. The Plan must include:

- Gender monitoring strategy
- Affirmative actions to improve the access of women producers to agricultural extension services
- Guidelines for the dissemination of information to women producers
- Amendments to regulations on quotas for the participation of women in boards of directors
- Strategies to promote the inclusion of women extension workers within the Producer Association
- Culture-sensitive budgeting to identify funded activities and/or projects aimed at improving the conditions of women producers
- Protection against sexual harassment, violence, and sexual harassment and any form of discrimination
- Engagement with relevant governmental institutions to develop initiatives that benefit women producers

3.5.4 Fedegan, Fereshteh: Strengthen the (existing) Gender Action Plan that mainstreams gender within the Association’s policies and practices.

- Gender monitoring strategy
- Affirmative actions to improve the access of women producers to agricultural extension services
- Guidelines for the dissemination of information to women producers
- Amendments to regulations on quotas for the participation of women in boards of directors
- Strategies to promote the inclusion of women extension workers within the Producer Association
- Culture-sensitive budgeting to identify funded activities and/or projects aimed at improving the conditions of women producers
- Protection against sexual harassment, violence, and sexual harassment and any form of discrimination

3.5.5 Fereshteh: Strengthen the (existing) Gender Action Plan that mainstreams gender within the Association’s policies and practices.

- Prevention of sexual harassment in workplaces and sexual harassment and any form of discrimination
